# Legendary Arena -- Development Roadmap (Mindmap)

> **Checklist rule (hard):** one line per item; status-first; no subordinate clauses; no file lists / commit hashes / decisions / dependency prose. If the line forces the reader to *read* before answering "done / drafted / blocked", it's still wrong.
>
> **Status vocabulary (closed set):**
> `✅ Done` · `🚧 In Progress` · `📝 Drafted` (WP file authored; awaiting execution) · `📦 Queued` (deps met; WP file not yet authored) · `⏸ Blocked` (dep unmet) · `📝 Placeholder` (forward-looking only).
>
> All audit detail (per-WP file lists, commit hashes, decision IDs, baselines, deltas, post-mortems) lives in `docs/ai/work-packets/WORK_INDEX.md`, the per-WP files under `docs/ai/work-packets/`, and `docs/ai/STATUS.md`. This file is navigation — not a record.

        ["WP-439 ✅ Server runtime-health signal on the operator dashboard (server + dashboard; answers do-we-need-to-cluster with data. Came out of the 2026-07-27 clustering discussion — a deploy log showed Render setting WEB_CONCURRENCY=2, which is INERT today because the server runs a single node process, no cluster/throng wrapper. Whether to cluster to use the second core should be decided by EVIDENCE of single-process CPU saturation, not a hunch, and Node's standard saturation signal is event-loop delay: a rising p99 while one core maxes and the others idle is the clustering-would-help tell. New admin-gated GET /api/dash/system/runtime returns a live RuntimeHealthSnapshot — capturedAt, uptimeSeconds, cpuCount, cpuPercent, eventLoopDelayMs{mean,p50,p99,max}, memoryRssMb, webConcurrency — sampled ON REQUEST from process/perf_hooks/os: a module-load monitorEventLoopDelay histogram read+reset per request so each reading is the delay distribution over the window since the last poll, and a process.cpuUsage() delta over an hrtime span held in module state, seeded at load so the first request has a baseline, normalised ÷cpuCount to a machine-capacity percent so a pure-JS process caps near 100/cpuCount, one core, and approaching that ceiling while other cores idle is the tell. Reads NO database, NO registry, NO engine — the shared pool is threaded to the route ONLY for the requireAdminSession gate; server layer clock/CPU reads are allowed, the no-clock rule is an engine rule. Metric-math is PURE, buildRuntimeHealthSnapshot / computeCpuPercent / parseWebConcurrency, unit-tested with fixed inputs; only the gatherer getRuntimeHealthSnapshot is impure. Investigation 2026-07-29 found the root cause is NOT scheme setup, buildInitialGameState leaves G.counters empty and evaluateEndgame null at initial state for every scheme including the two failing ones, and it is NOT a zero or undefined loss threshold. It is a simulation reverse-shuffle artifact: buildVillainDeck lexically sorts the assembled deck before shuffling for deterministic pre-shuffle order, the virtual scheme-twist-slug-NN ids sort LAST since s beats m h c b, and the simulation and fixture setup path builds its SetupContext via makeMockCtx whose random Shuffle is deck to reversed-deck, a deterministic reverse the unit tests rely on to prove shuffle ran, so reversing a list whose twists are at the bottom stacks all 8 scheme-twists on TOP of the villain deck, verified top 8 are scheme-twist 07 down to 00. midtown-bank-robbery and negative-zone-prison-breakout are the only two core schemes whose twist resolver CHAINS extra villain reveals via performVillainReveal, so the turn-1 twist reveals the next twist which chains the next, cascading through all 8 clustered twists in one reveal, and schemeTwistHandler increments schemeTwistCount each time until it hits the lossThreshold 8 doom-clock proxy from D-24178 and evaluateEndgame returns scheme-wins at turn 0. Non-chaining schemes are front-loaded too, one top-of-deck twist per turn losing around turn 8, so the reverse mock systematically depresses ALL simulated win rates including the WP-452 0.0 percent baseline, which this WP does not predict the post-fix number for, the executor measures it. Production is unaffected since boardgame.io supplies the real seeded PRNG shuffle in the live engine. No committed PAR artifact or CI gate exists so there is no PAR re-pin. Adds a registry-free regression test asserting the seeded setup shuffle is neither identity nor reverse and does not cluster scheme-twist ids contiguously at the top, and is deterministic per seed.         ["WP-411 ✅ Set ctx.gameover at end of game (top-level endIf; CRITICAL latent bug found live-verifying WP-407/408. A clean prod win (J8rw6ziU4Xg, Magneto's four tactics defeated) returned 404 match_not_finished; a DB read showed both metadata.gameover AND state.ctx.gameover null after a decisive victory. Root cause: the endgame check is a PHASE-level endIf on the play phase, which in boardgame.io only ends the phase (-> the empty end phase) and does NOT set ctx.gameover. LegendaryGame has no top-level endIf and no events.endGame() anywhere; the phase-endIf comment even asserts the wrong belief that a truthy phase-endIf return becomes ctx.gameover (true only for a top-level endIf). So NO match ever registers gameover, and every completed-match consumer is dead in prod: competitive submission (WP-338 -> competitive_scores empty), result-LAGN (WP-406), Hall of Legends (WP-407/408), match summaries (D-24169); every finished-match query returns 0 rows. No test caught it because the suite asserts the pure evaluateEndgame(G) function, never the boardgame.io wiring that sets ctx.gameover. Fix: add top-level endIf ({G}) => evaluateEndgame(G) ?? undefined; remove the redundant phase endIf; keep the four phases (lobby/setup/play/end, pinned by game.test.ts). Tests close the gap: a LegendaryGame.endIf unit assertion + an engine-runner integration test that plays to a real gameover. Determinism scaffold-gated: no G mutation so finalStateHash unchanged for an identical G; games now terminate at the win/loss point, so any fixture recorded past it is re-recorded via the canonical recorder + reasoned re-pin, never silent. No server edit (server already reads metadata.gameover). User-visible surface = none directly (engine wiring) but unblocks the completed-match pipeline; D-24026 live-verify REQUIRED, also unblocks WP-407/408's populated-roster verify. Reserves D-24223; EC-446; drafted 2026-07-21. DONE 2026-07-21: top-level endIf added, phase endIf removed; AC-3 wiring test (framework sets ctx.gameover via boardgame.io reducer InitializeGame+CreateGameReducer) realized in game.test.ts NOT engine-runner — runSimulation re-implements the turn loop + calls evaluateEndgame directly, never instantiates boardgame.io, so it has no ctx.gameover to assert (EC-446 Execution Amendment); runMatch.test.ts got an honest end-to-end termination smoke instead; engine-only, no server edit. build 0; game-engine 2053->2055/0 +AC-1+AC-3; engine-runner 19->20/0; whole-repo --no-bail clean; finalStateHash + PRE_WP080_HASH UNCHANGED (no G mutation, AC-4 re-record a verified no-op); D-24223 Active; AC-6 D-24026 live-verify operator-pending on the engine deploy)"]

        ["WP-414 ✅ Bot-ally stall surfacing + restart revival (server; fixes a reported prod freeze on play.legendary-arena.com. A bot-ally match froze on the bot's turn while the operator ran concurrent sessions that merge to main, which triggers a Render redeploy that destroys the in-process BotAllyDriver mid-match. A live driver can never freeze forever (it faults within BOT_MAX_MOVE_STEPS_PER_TURN), so a lasting freeze means the driver is gone (restart) or was persisted faulted and never revived — and rehydrateBotAllyDrivers today revives only status=active rows, while the fault_message it writes is exposed by no endpoint, so the human on seat 0 waits forever with no signal. Fix, server half: a read-only GET /api/match/:matchId/bot-ally-status (guest by capability, side-table-only per D-24095, absent for a non-bot match) returning driving/status/message; the driver retries a wedged turn once from a fresh fetch before faulting; restart revival re-attaches a still-live faulted/exhausted match bounded by a new revive_count column (MAX_REVIVALS=3) so a genuinely-stuck match settles to a surfaced faulted instead of looping every deploy. Migration 036 adds revive_count, additive and idempotent; no server.mjs edit (route registers inside registerBotAllyRoutes, revival is already wired). New guest GET so api-endpoints.md gains a row. User-visible on play.legendary-arena.com, D-24026 required. Pairs with WP-415 (the client banner). Reserves D-24229 and D-24230; EC-449; drafted 2026-07-22)"]
        ["WP-415 ✅ Bot-ally stall banner (client; The client half of the bot-ally freeze fix: a useBotAllyStatus composable consumes WP-414's GET /api/match/:matchId/bot-ally-status on the play surface — probe once on mount, poll only a real bot-ally match (absent stops it), stop on a terminal status and on unmount, fail-soft on a fetch error. When the bot ally stopped abnormally (driving false, status not completed or absent) a co-op-framed BotAllyStallBanner renders the server's public-safe message verbatim plus a client-only Return-to-lobby escape (no new or destructive endpoint). A healthy match and a normally-completed match show nothing; a network blip is not a stopped bot. Mounted once at the D-16501 PlayViewport play-root, the WP-410/412 01.5 wiring precedent; no runtime registry/server import; section 23(b) co-op framing on every string. User-visible on play.legendary-arena.com, D-24026 required. Reserves D-24231; EC-450; drafted 2026-07-22)"]
        ["WP-418 ✅ New-version refresh prompt + reconnect-gap audit (client; kills the deploy-mid-match freeze. When a deploy lands mid-match, Cloudflare Pages swaps the hashed asset chunks the open tab references, so a lazy-loaded chunk 404s and the board freezes or paints blank tiles — the classic stale-SPA-after-deploy, not engine logic. The reconnect stack (D-24232, WP-311, WP-312, D-24234) re-anchors match state but cannot fix a stale JS bundle; only a page reload does. Fix, client only: A) the build emits a tiny version.json ({ gitSha }) via a Vite plugin reusing the existing gitSha capture — no second git call, no npm dep, and configureServer serves it in dev — polled cache:no-store and compared against the baked __GIT_SHA__ by the pure isNewerBuildAvailable; B) a window vite:preloadError catch flips the flag immediately and preventDefaults the white-screen. Triggers on mount, tab-focus, socket reconnect (a WP-311 connection store isConnected false-to-true edge, reused rather than a second socket listener), and a ~60s backstop. UpdateAvailableBanner.vue — read-only, role=status, glyph plus text, keyboard-reachable — renders a user-initiated Refresh now (location.reload, prop-drilled from PlayViewport) plus a dismiss; never an auto or forced reload (that would discard an in-progress action; an auto-reload-when-safe path is a deferred sub-fork). Fail-soft: a failed or missing version.json shows nothing. Reconnect-gap audit: D-24232 still covers the pure transport-drop, a stale bundle is out of resync's reach by design, and the resync stack is not refactored. Pure arena-client plus one build-config emit; no engine/server/registry change; section 21 N/A. Reserves D-24238; EC-453; drafted+executed 2026-07-24)"]
        ["WP-419 ✅ Bot-ally status liveness + strand-to-faulted (server; fixes a silent bot-ally freeze found in a live diagnostic, match vMxtCoOZDFj, frozen on the bot's turn 1 while GET /api/match/:matchId/bot-ally-status reported driving:true and status:active. The bot driver's fault machinery is bounded, so a match stuck active for 30+ minutes means no driver is actually running: the in-process BotAllyDriver was destroyed by a redeploy, plausibly WP-418's own Render redeploy, and active is merely its creation status, never overwritten. Two defects turned that driver-loss into a silent permanent freeze. One, the status endpoint derived driving from the DB active flag with no liveness check, so a dead-but-active match reported healthy and the WP-415 banner, which needs driving:false, never showed. Two, a match that burns all MAX_REVIVALS revivals without completing a turn is excluded from revival yet left active, un-revived and un-surfaced. Fix, server only: A) driving equals row.status active AND botAllyDrivers.has(matchId), where botAllyDrivers is the process live-driver registry, so a driverless active match reports driving:false and the banner surfaces; B) rehydrateBotAllyDrivers now calls settleStrandedActiveMatches, which reads status active AND revive_count at or above MAX_REVIVALS and settles each driverless row to completed or faulted with the public-safe BOT_FAULTED_MESSAGE, skipping a row this boot just revived because it has a live driver. Untouched: the revival-cap read and increment logic that the 2026-07-23 OOM-loop hotfix owns; exhausted is not stranded; recovering a genuinely-drivable capped match and a multi-instance DB heartbeat are named follow-ups, single-instance assumed. No determinism, persistence, response-shape, or auth change; section 21 N/A. Reserves D-24239; EC-454; drafted+executed 2026-07-24)"]
        ["WP-420 ✅ Deploy-aware bot-ally revival (server; the recovery follow-up WP-419 named. WP-419 surfaces a driverless bot-ally match and faults a cap-stranded one; this recovers a match that was healthy and being driven when a clean deploy destroyed its in-process driver — the observed vMxtCoOZDFj case, a match that would play fine if the driver returned but burns all MAX_REVIVALS revivals across restarts without completing a turn and then faults. Design: distinguish lost-to-a-clean-deploy, recover, from crashed or wedged, do not loop. A graceful SIGTERM shutdown in index.mjs, which is what Render sends on a redeploy, marks the matches currently in botAllyDrivers via a new additive shutdown_interrupted column, migration 037, before the pool closes, best-effort. Boot revival includes a shutdown_interrupted row regardless of the cap, revive_count < MAX_REVIVALS OR shutdown_interrupted, and clears the flag on re-registration, so a clean shutdown earns exactly one past-cap revival; a recovered drivable match then completes a turn and D-24233 resets revive_count, while a match that still cannot complete a turn and is not cleanly interrupted again strands to faulted per WP-419. An ungraceful loss, OOM or crash with no SIGTERM and so no flag, is never free-revived: it keeps WP-414's cap and WP-419's strand-to-faulted, so the 2026-07-23 OOM restart loop cannot return because its terminator is a crash with no clean shutdown, exactly the capped case. Rejected: a partial-progress reset, which would reopen the loop. Server orchestration plus an additive side-table column, D-24095 store-only; no engine or registry change; section 21 N/A. Reserves D-24240; EC-455; drafted+executed 2026-07-24. Executed: migration 037 shutdown_interrupted; index.mjs SIGTERM marks the botAllyDrivers matches before pool close, best-effort; readRevivableBotAllyMatches OR-extended with shutdown_interrupted; markBotAllyMatchRevived clears the flag on revival)"]
        ["WP-424 ✅ Stop bot-ally drivers on SIGTERM, deploy-overlap freeze (server; fixes a silent bot-ally freeze that recurs on essentially every deploy catching a bot mid-turn — live diag DBlXvBs_WXA, frozen on the bot's turn 15 right after the bot fought a villain, the turn never ended, while the status reported driving:true and status:active. The deploy log named it: after the new instance re-registered the driver, boardgame.io's Master repeatedly rejected the bot's move, invalid stateID was 273 expected 274 playerID 1 action fightVillain, again 288 and 289 fifty-eight seconds later — an off-by-one _stateID race, two writers driving the same bot seat. Root cause: the SIGTERM handler in index.mjs marks the driven matches shutdown_interrupted per WP-420 but never stops their drivers; Render's rolling deploy boots the NEW instance, which revives and drives those matches, BEFORE it SIGTERMs the OLD one, whose handler then blocks in httpServer.close draining the human's long-lived Socket.IO connection — so the old instance's drivers keep polling every 250ms and submitting the bot's moves for the entire termination-grace window until SIGKILL. Two instances driving the same seat race on _stateID; the slow chain-triggering fightVillain loses repeatedly, never lands, the turn wedges, and because a driver is still registered driving:true, WP-419's stall banner which needs driving:false never fires — a silent frozen board. Fix, server only: A) new stopAllBotAllyDrivers() snapshots botAllyDrivers.values() and calls each driver.stop(), clearing the timer, setting stopped, de-registering; B) call it in the SIGTERM handler AFTER markInProgressBotAllyMatchesInterrupted reads the driven-match keys, because stopping first would empty the map; C) an in-flight tick bails on driver.stopped, attemptBotTurn returns at the loop top and runTick skips the post-turn side-table write. The old instance ends its participation at SIGTERM; WP-420's mark still lets the new instance revive the match as the sole writer, so fightVillain lands and the turn completes. Untouched: WP-420 mark-and-revive, the status route and driving, the client banner, determinism and persistence. Deferred: a cross-instance ownership guard, a DB advisory lock or driver_owner plus heartbeat, the durable multi-instance fix that closes the residual boot-to-SIGTERM window; single-instance steady state assumed. No determinism, persistence, response-shape, or auth change; section 21 N/A. Renumbered from WP-423/EC-458/D-24243 after a concurrent session landed those numbers first, hugo-version-upgrade, PR 1000. Reserves D-24244; EC-459; Lightweight lane drafted+executed 2026-07-25. User-visible on play.legendary-arena.com, D-24026 required, operator-pending on the deploy eyeball)"]
        ["WP-426 ✅ Bot-ally driver survives a transient Postgres blip (server; another silent bot-ally freeze, this one on a build that already carries WP-424 — live diag nZn_U4QO-hr, frozen on the bot's turn while the status reported driving:true and status:active. Root cause from the server deploy log: Postgres briefly went unreachable after a deploy, bgio-store fetch for the match ECONNREFUSED on port 5432 across all 4 retries, still failing after 4 transient retries keeping the server up returning empty, immediately followed by bot-ally match failed to persist teardown status completed. The bgio store's resilientFetch returns EMPTY, not a throw, when it exhausts retries against an unreachable database, the number-930 keep-the-server-up behavior; but the driver's runTick treated an empty fetchState as match vanished from the store and tore down as completed — so a transient DB blip, a Render database restart or instability around a deploy, killed the driver mid-match, and the teardown's own status write then also failed against the same dead database, leaving the side-table row active with no live driver, a silent freeze the human sees as a frozen board, self-healing only on a later restart's revival and re-looping if the DB stays flaky. An empty fetch is AMBIGUOUS, it means either a reaped match or an unreachable DB, and the driver assumed the former on the FIRST empty. Fix, server only: a new emptyFetchPolls counter plus a tolerateEmptyFetch helper, increment on each empty, reset to 0 on any non-empty fetch, and only teardown completed once it reaches BOT_MAX_EMPTY_FETCH_POLLS, 90, about several minutes since each empty tick already carries the store's own 1.8 second retry. BOTH empty sites funnel through it, the top-of-tick state-is-null and the mid-turn driveBotTurn vanished result, split out from game-over. A DB outage now recovers with the driver still registered and resumes driving; only a match that stays empty past the whole window, genuinely reaped which is rare for a live driver whose gameover or idle-abandon teardown fires first, tears down. Untouched: WP-419 liveness driving, WP-420 mark-and-revive, WP-424 SIGTERM stop, and the store's empty-to-stay-up contract, making it throw was rejected as a wider blast radius; determinism and persistence. Flagged separately: the infra trigger, why Postgres goes ECONNREFUSED around deploys, a deploy-coupled DB restart or residual instability despite the basic-1gb move number-932, is an operational investigation not this code fix. No determinism, persistence, response-shape, or auth change; section 21 N/A. Reserves D-24247; EC-461; Lightweight lane drafted+executed 2026-07-25.         ["WP-433 ✅ Bot-ally turn-fault observability, log the reason and pending-choice state (server; closes the silent-fault observability gap. attemptBotTurn in botAllyDriver.mjs has four fault-return sites and only the decision-threw one logged, so the other three, policy returned no move, the offered move never advanced the _stateID, and the per-turn step cap, faulted SILENTLY.         ["WP-437 ✅ Bot-ally cross-instance ownership guard, deploy-overlap two-writer freeze (server; closes the residual freeze WP-424 and D-24244 explicitly deferred. WP-424 stopped the OLD instance's drivers at SIGTERM, but on a rolling Render deploy the NEW instance boots, revives a match's BotAllyDriver, and starts submitting the bot's moves BEFORE Render SIGTERMs the OLD one, so for the termination-grace window TWO instances drive the same bot seat and race on boardgame.io's _stateID, invalid stateID was N expected N+1; the bot's move never lands, the turn livelocks, and because a driver is registered the status route reports driving true status active so neither WP-419's banner which needs driving false nor WP-433's fault log fires, a silent frozen board, live match Sk1ASNTkGSz, same signature as the WP-424 case DBlXvBs_WXA. Fix, server only, D-24256: a per-match ownership LEASE in legendary.match_bot_ally, migration 038 additive driver_owner text plus heartbeat_at timestamptz, NULL-default and idempotent. A new botAllyOwnership.mjs holds SERVER_INSTANCE_ID, a per-process crypto.randomUUID, BOT_ALLY_LEASE_TTL_MS 15000, an atomic claim-or-renew acquireOrRenewBotAllyLease, claimable when unowned or mine or never-heartbeated or stale past the TTL, a fresh peer lease is not, and releaseBotAllyLeasesForOwner. The driver arbitrates COOPERATIVELY at each 250ms poll tick, top of runTick via an OPTIONAL deps.renewOrAcquireLease: it drives only while it holds the lease, YIELDS the tick to a live peer submitting nothing so no _stateID race, and skips the tick on a lease-check throw. The existing poll loop IS the retry, so the newly-revived driver takes over the instant the old owner releases, SIGTERM releaseBotAllyLeasesForOwner in index.mjs is a near-instant handoff, or its heartbeat expires, the roughly 15s crash TTL. Design, D-24256: Option B, the heartbeat lease, over Option A, a pg advisory lock, because the single shared max 10 pool cannot pin a client per driver for the driver's lifetime without starving every other server surface, and a session-scoped advisory lock requires exactly that pinned connection; Option B uses pooled queries, composes with the existing side-table and WP-420 SIGTERM machinery, and is observable, driver_owner and heartbeat_at are directly queryable and handoffs log. Behavioural NO-OP for a single instance, it always wins its own lease, and the optional dep keeps every pre-WP-437 unit test and single-instance prod byte-unchanged. TTL 15s never false-expires a live owner, which renews every tick, yet frees a crashed owner with no clean SIGTERM within a bounded window; a single turn exceeding 15s under a sustained DB outage is a documented residual, still bounded by the fault machinery and the stale-stateID reject. Untouched: the 250ms poll, fault and retry budgets, MAX_REVIVALS, WP-419 liveness, WP-420 revival, WP-424 SIGTERM-stop, WP-426 empty-tolerance; the human is NEVER gated, the lease governs only which instance drives the bot seat. Ownership state is side-table only per D-24095, never G or ctx and never the bgio blob, the same data class as revive_count and shutdown_interrupted. driver_owner is NOT surfaced on the public guest status route, it stays driving status message, so section 21 and D-11804 N/A, no HTTP shape change, ownership is observed via the DB and logs. Section 17 Vision, section 11 lifecycle and section 14 reliability, no conflict. Bot-ally suite plus 9, 4 botAllyOwnership.test.ts, instance-id and TTL shape, claim returns true, peer-fresh returns false, release by owner, and 5 driver lease cases, owner drives, peer yields, cooperative handoff, lease throw skips, no-dep back-compat; server suite 948/0 plus 158 DB-skipped; pnpm -r build 0; migration 038 applied and re-applied idempotent against a local Postgres and the claim-or-renew exercised live, claim unowned 1, peer fresh 0, stale past TTL 1, release then re-claim 1. Reserves D-24256; EC-472; standard lane drafted+executed 2026-07-26. User-visible on play.legendary-arena.com, D-24026 live-verify operator-pending, deploy twice in quick succession mid bot-ally match, the class only reproduces under real overlapping deploys. Hard-deps WP-375, WP-419, WP-420, WP-424, WP-426, WP-433)"]

        ["WP-423 ✅ Coordinated Hugo version upgrade — ewiki build surface (infrastructure / shared tooling; bumps the engine monorepo's ONE Hugo site, apps/wiki-viewer the ewiki, from 0.135.0 to a current Hugo Extended release, recommended 0.161.1 to match sibling legendary-arena-lab's production pin, and adds a CI page-presence guard so a silent sub-page drop fails the build instead of shipping a 404. Modeled on the legendary-arena-lab outage: CI and Cloudflare Pages were pinned 0.149.0 while local dev ran 0.161.1, a newer-syntax files glob was UNKNOWN on 0.149.0 so the wiki mount silently dropped and every sub-page 404'd in production while hugo --minify exited 0 and CI stayed green; lab PRs 13 guard, 14 pin, 15 config-modernize-after-floor, 12 headers. Discovered, not assumed, is the spine and this repo differs in four load-bearing ways. One, exactly one Hugo site here, a grep of workflows, render.yaml, config files, package.json, .tool-versions, netlify.toml, and wrangler found no second site. Two, CI, the wiki-viewer.yml workflow using peaceiris/actions-hugo v3 extended, and the Render deploy, render.yaml buildCommand curling hugo_extended of cat .hugo-version, already read the same apps/wiki-viewer/.hugo-version file, so the lab's CI-versus-dashboard drift cannot recur and the only drift surface is local dev with its unpinned binary. Three, there is no Cloudflare Pages Hugo build in this repo, the Pages and dashboard-HUGO_VERSION and floor-0.146.0 material in wiki/hugo-onboarding.md documents the marketing site in the separate C:\\www\\legendary-arena-com repo whose upgrade is a companion WP there, out of scope. Four, hugo.toml uses a single-directory content projection with no Modules and no files or module.mounts keys, so the lab's exact silent-mount-drop is structurally absent and the page-presence guard is added as defense-in-depth beside the existing determinism and JS-free gates. Lockstep invariant: the pin lives in .hugo-version only, no version literal in the workflow or render.yaml.
